## Module 18

**Tissue pair**: brain and lung  
**Genes (5)**: CNDP2, NDUFS4, NDUFV2, PIK3R2, TRNT1

### Function

The strongest supported interpretation is mitochondrial energy metabolism linked to phosphoinositide signaling. The only GO/KEGG term surviving global correction is (KEGG Non-alcoholic fatty liver disease - Homo sapiens (human); q_global = 0.0273), non-alcoholic fatty liver disease, driven by PIK3R2, NDUFS4, and NDUFV2. This pathway label should not be read as evidence that the module specifically causes liver disease; it is more plausibly capturing shared processes involving mitochondrial respiratory function and PI3K-regulated metabolic signaling. NDUFS4 and NDUFV2 are established components of mitochondrial complex I and support NADH-to-ubiquinone electron transfer, aerobic respiration, proton-motive-force generation, and ATP production. PIK3R2 is a regulatory subunit of PI3K and is associated with insulin-receptor signaling, intracellular glucose homeostasis, autophagy regulation, and broader signal transduction. The repeated supplementary STRING associations with non-alcoholic fatty liver disease, prion disease, and Alzheimer disease (4 supplementary STRING terms; within-query FDR 0.0055–0.0245) are concordant with mitochondrial and metabolic biology, but they are supplementary rather than globally corrected primary evidence. TRNT1 and CNDP2 broaden the module toward mitochondrial RNA maturation, RNA surveillance, proteolysis, and glutathione-related metabolism. Because only one global GO/KEGG term passed correction and the remaining genes lack globally significant annotations, the module should be interpreted as a compact metabolic and mitochondrial co-abundance program rather than as a disease-specific pathway.

**Cell composition.** The module is not dominated by secreted proteins, and HPA reports no secreted members. Its members show low tissue specificity, with protein detected in both brain and lung for every gene, arguing against a simple tissue-restricted program. HPA also reports low immune-cell specificity for most members and no immune-cell detection for PIK3R2, with no enriched blood-cell category reported; this provides little support for a blood-contamination explanation. CNDP2 and NDUFV2 show cell-type-enhanced patterns involving enterocytes or related epithelial populations, while PIK3R2 is enriched across several neural and glial cell types. These observations suggest that the shared signal may reflect broadly distributed cellular metabolism, with possible contributions from epithelial and neural cell populations, rather than a plasma-protein or blood-cell module. Cell composition and sample composition remain possible contributors because co-abundance data alone do not establish cell autonomy.

**GO/KEGG enrichment**: 1 terms pass global BH (q_global < 0.05).

| Term | q_global | genes |
|---|---|---|
| KEGG Non-alcoholic fatty liver disease - Homo sapiens (human) | 0.0273 | NDUFS4, NDUFV2, PIK3R2 |

### STRING and hu.MAP

STRING provides a very specific known relationship between NDUFS4 and NDUFV2, with very strong confidence. This is biologically coherent because both are mitochondrial complex I subunits. No STRING partners are reported for PIK3R2, TRNT1, or CNDP2 within the supplied interaction result. hu.MAP independently places NDUFS4 and NDUFV2 together in multiple complex-I-related assemblies (hu.MAP huMAP3_02819.1) (hu.MAP huMAP3_14018.1) (hu.MAP huMAP3_06397.1) (hu.MAP huMAP3_09982.1) (hu.MAP huMAP3_09567.1) (hu.MAP huMAP3_14878.1), with NDUFS4 and NDUFV2 being the recurring module members while the listed complexes contain many additional respiratory-chain subunits. Thus, the module contains a validated respiratory-chain pair embedded in larger known complexes, but most co-abundance edges are not supported by the supplied STRING or hu.MAP relationships. The network therefore combines a well-established mitochondrial core with potentially context-dependent associations involving PIK3R2, TRNT1, and CNDP2.

**STRING interactions (score >= 0.7)**: 1 of 10 gene pairs: NDUFS4–NDUFV2 (0.999)
**STRING functional enrichment (supplementary, within-query FDR)**: KEGG Non-alcoholic fatty liver disease (FDR = 0.0055, 3 genes); WikiPathways Nonalcoholic fatty liver disease (FDR = 0.0121, 3 genes); KEGG Prion disease (FDR = 0.0209, 3 genes); KEGG Alzheimer disease (FDR = 0.0245, 3 genes)
**hu.MAP 3.0 complexes with >= 2 module members**: huMAP3_02819.1 (NDUFS4, NDUFV2; 2/34); huMAP3_14018.1 (NDUFS4, NDUFV2; 2/48); huMAP3_06397.1 (NDUFS4, NDUFV2; 2/49); huMAP3_09982.1 (NDUFS4, NDUFV2; 2/49); huMAP3_09567.1 (NDUFS4, NDUFV2; 2/52); huMAP3_14878.1 (NDUFS4, NDUFV2; 2/53)

### Novelty

The module is not wholly novel at the molecular-mechanism level because the NDUFS4-NDUFV2 relationship is strongly supported by STRING and both genes repeatedly occur together in hu.MAP complex assemblies. The respiratory-chain interpretation is therefore established rather than a new discovery. A potentially novel aspect is the cross-tissue co-abundance configuration linking this complex-I pair with PIK3R2, TRNT1, and CNDP2 in brain and lung, because the supplied resources do not report corresponding STRING partners or hu.MAP membership for those genes. However, absence from STRING or hu.MAP does not prove novelty: those resources emphasize physical interactions and curated complexes, whereas co-abundance can arise from shared regulation, cell composition, metabolic state, or tissue-specific coordination. The defensible conclusion is that the module represents a potentially new cross-tissue co-abundance pattern built around a known mitochondrial core, not a wholly new molecular complex or pathway.

**Hypothesis.** Hypothesis: in both brain and lung, cellular energetic demand or metabolic stress may coordinate mitochondrial complex-I activity with PI3K-regulated nutrient and growth signaling. In this model, NDUFS4 and NDUFV2 form the respiratory core; PIK3R2 links that core to insulin, glucose-homeostasis, autophagy, and growth signaling; TRNT1 may reflect the requirement for mitochondrial tRNA maturation and translational quality control; and CNDP2 may reflect associated peptide and glutathione metabolism. The observed co-abundance could therefore represent coordinated maintenance of ATP production, mitochondrial gene-expression capacity, redox balance, and nutrient-responsive signaling. This remains a hypothesis: the fact sheet does not establish directionality, physical interaction among all members, causation, or whether the pattern is driven by cell-state composition.

This is the least externally validated member of the proposed mechanism and requires independent experimental testing.

## Module 19

**Tissue pair**: brain and lung  
**Genes (6)**: DIS3L2, HIP1R, PTGR2, PC, TRAPPC12, TRAPPC8

### Function

The module most plausibly reflects coordinated intracellular trafficking and organelle organization, centered on TRAPP-related ER-to-Golgi transport. TRAPPC12 and TRAPPC8 are both annotated as TRAPP components involved in Golgi organization, ER-to-Golgi vesicle-mediated transport, and COPII vesicle coat assembly. The remaining genes connect this trafficking axis to prostaglandin metabolism through PTGR2, mitochondrial anaplerotic and biosynthetic metabolism through PC, RNA turnover through DIS3L2, and endocytosis and actin organization through HIP1R. However, this is an interpretation of the gene annotations and network topology, not a globally significant enrichment result: no GO or KEGG term passed global correction, and no STRING functional-enrichment term was reported. The co-abundance graph is therefore more consistent with a small, biologically mixed trafficking-associated module than with a confidently assigned single canonical pathway.

**Cell composition.** The module is not dominated by secreted or plasma proteins: none of the genes is classified as secreted. Most genes have low tissue specificity, while two are tissue enhanced, with liver and testis represented among the enriched tissues. All proteins were detected in both brain and lung. Single-cell annotations are more informative than tissue-level annotations: several genes are enhanced in neural or glial populations, while others are enhanced in renal, epithelial, metabolic, or immune-associated populations. Three genes show immune-cell enhancement, including basophil and B-cell annotations, but the module is not uniformly blood-cell specific. This mixed pattern argues against treating the shared brain-lung signal as a purely brain-specific program or as a straightforward plasma-protein or blood-contamination signature. It may instead reflect broadly distributed cellular machinery, with contributions from shared cell types or cellular-state composition in the sampled tissues.

**GO/KEGG enrichment**: 0 terms pass global BH (q_global < 0.05).

### STRING and hu.MAP

STRING provides one high-confidence relationship, between TRAPPC12 and TRAPPC8, and reports no supplementary functional-enrichment term. This is important because the strongest direct network support links the two TRAPP-related genes rather than connecting all module members into one established interaction network. hu.MAP independently places TRAPPC12 and TRAPPC8 together in three reported complexes, (hu.MAP huMAP3_04127.1), (hu.MAP huMAP3_07907.1), and (hu.MAP huMAP3_12177.1). In each case they are only a subset of a much larger TRAPP-related complex, whose other members include multiple TRAPP subunits. Thus the module contains a reproducible known TRAPP-associated pair, but the other genes are not established members of those listed complexes. The network may capture co-abundance between a core trafficking pair and genes associated with metabolism, RNA processing, or endocytosis rather than a single physically assembled complex.

**STRING interactions (score >= 0.7)**: 1 of 15 gene pairs: TRAPPC12–TRAPPC8 (0.999)
**hu.MAP 3.0 complexes with >= 2 module members**: huMAP3_04127.1 (TRAPPC12, TRAPPC8; 2/15); huMAP3_07907.1 (TRAPPC12, TRAPPC8; 2/18); huMAP3_12177.1 (TRAPPC12, TRAPPC8; 2/20)

### Novelty

The module may represent a novel cross-tissue co-abundance pattern, but the evidence does not establish a new molecular complex or a new pathway. The known TRAPPC12–TRAPPC8 relationship and their repeated hu.MAP placement show that part of the signal recapitulates established biology. Conversely, the absence of reported STRING or hu.MAP support for the links involving PTGR2, PC, DIS3L2, and HIP1R means those co-abundance relationships are not already documented there, but absence from these resources does not prove novelty. A cautious conclusion is that the potentially novel result is the coordinated abundance pattern across brain and lung, especially the association of the TRAPP pair with the other genes, rather than discovery of an entirely unknown interaction.

**Hypothesis.** Hypothesis: a shared cellular program involving secretory-pathway organization and vesicle trafficking may coordinate with membrane uptake, lipid mediator metabolism, mitochondrial anaplerosis, and RNA quality control in selected brain and lung cell populations. TRAPPC12 and TRAPPC8 could form the central trafficking axis; HIP1R could connect this axis to endocytic and actin-dependent membrane organization; PTGR2 could reflect modulation of prostaglandin signaling; PC could reflect metabolic support; and DIS3L2 could reflect coupling to RNA turnover or proliferative state. This hypothesis is consistent with the annotations and the co-abundance structure, but it is not demonstrated by global GO or KEGG enrichment, by STRING functional enrichment, or by direct interaction evidence for the peripheral genes.
